**Tool Result:** `toolu_01TEpqYea5uAqXuc3RPzVejm`

```
1) TL;DR

Use the same pattern you already have: `std::thread` + `std::sync::mpsc`, no Tokio. Add a single long‑lived syntax worker thread fed by a request channel, and send results back via the existing `msg_tx` as `Msg::SyntaxUpdated { … }`. Debounce by scheduling a 50 ms timer command and gate both starting the parse and applying results with a per‑document revision/generation number.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### 0. High‑level shape

End‑to‑end flow per edit:

1. User types → `Msg::Document(..)` → `update()` mutates document, bumps its `revision`, and returns `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse { doc_id, revision }`.
2. `process_cmd()` handles `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse` by spawning a tiny timer thread: `sleep(50ms)` → send `Msg::SyntaxParseReady { doc_id, revision }` via `msg_tx`.
3. `update()` handles `Msg::SyntaxParseReady`:
   - If `revision != doc.current_revision` → ignore (edit happened since the timer, so this is stale).
   - Else → snapshot document text and return `Cmd::RunSyntaxParse { doc_id, revision, text }`.
4. `process_cmd()` sends `ParseRequest { doc_id, revision, text }` to a dedicated parser worker thread via an `mpsc::Sender`.
5. Parser thread does CPU work, then sends `Msg::SyntaxUpdated { doc_id, revision, highlights }` back on `msg_tx`.
6. `update()` handles `Msg::SyntaxUpdated`:
   - If `revision != doc.current_revision` → ignore (results from stale parse).
   - Else → write highlights into the document and return `Cmd::Redraw`.

This gives you:

- Non‑blocking parse.
- Debounced start (50 ms quiet period).
- Logical cancellation of stale parses via revision checks.
- Integration into the existing `Cmd`/`Msg`/`update`/`process_cmd` pipeline.

---

### 1. Add syntax‑related messages and commands

First, extend `messages.rs` with syntax messages.

Assume you have some `DocumentId` and `SyntaxHighlightState` types in your model:

```rust
// messages.rs

use crate::model::{DocumentId, /* ... */};

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum SyntaxMsg {
    /// Debounced timer fired; we may start a parse for this revision.
    ParseReady {
        doc_id: DocumentId,
        revision: u64,
    },
    /// Background parse finished.
    Updated {
        doc_id: DocumentId,
        revision: u64,
        highlights: SyntaxHighlightState, // or Arc<...>
    },
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum Msg {
    // existing variants...
    Editor(EditorMsg),
    Document(DocumentMsg),
    Ui(UiMsg),
    Layout(LayoutMsg),
    App(AppMsg),

    // new
    Syntax(SyntaxMsg),
}
```

Then extend `commands.rs`:

```rust
// commands.rs

use crate::model::DocumentId;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub enum Cmd {
    #[default]
    None,
    Redraw,
    SaveFile { path: PathBuf, content: String },
    LoadFile { path: PathBuf },
    OpenInExplorer { path: PathBuf },
    OpenFileInEditor { path: PathBuf },
    Batch(Vec<Cmd>),

    ShowOpenFileDialog { allow_multi: bool, start_dir: Option<PathBuf> },
    ShowSaveFileDialog { suggested_path: Option<PathBuf> },
    ShowOpenFolderDialog { start_dir: Option<PathBuf> },

    // --- new syntax-related commands ---

    /// Start a debounce timer; after `delay_ms` send Syntax(ParseReady)
    DebouncedSyntaxParse {
        doc_id: DocumentId,
        revision: u64,
        delay_ms: u64,
    },

    /// Actually run the parser for this snapshot.
    RunSyntaxParse {
        doc_id: DocumentId,
        revision: u64,
        text: String,
    },
}
```

`needs_redraw()` can be conservative here:

```rust
impl Cmd {
    pub fn needs_redraw(&self) -> bool {
        match self {
            Cmd::None => false,
            Cmd::Redraw => true,
            Cmd::SaveFile { .. } => true,
            Cmd::LoadFile { .. } => true,
            Cmd::OpenInExplorer { .. } => true,
            Cmd::OpenFileInEditor { .. } => true,
            Cmd::Batch(cmds) => cmds.iter().any(|c| c.needs_redraw()),
            Cmd::ShowOpenFileDialog { .. } => false,
            Cmd::ShowSaveFileDialog { .. } => false,
            Cmd::ShowOpenFolderDialog { .. } => false,

            Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse { .. } => false,
            Cmd::RunSyntaxParse { .. } => false, // redraw happens on Syntax::Updated
        }
    }
}
```

---

### 2. Add a parser worker thread using `std::sync::mpsc`

Stay consistent with your existing `msg_tx/msg_rx` setup; no Tokio needed.

Define a request type and spawn the worker once in `App::new`:

```rust
// app.rs

use std::sync::mpsc;
use std::sync::mpsc::{Sender, Receiver};

// in App struct
pub struct App {
    // existing fields...
    msg_tx: Sender<Msg>,
    msg_rx: Receiver<Msg>,

    // new:
    syntax_tx: Sender<ParseRequest>,
}

struct ParseRequest {
    doc_id: DocumentId,
    revision: u64,
    text: String,
}

impl App {
    pub fn new(window_width: u32, window_height: u32, file_paths: Vec<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        let (msg_tx, msg_rx) = mpsc::channel();

        // spawn syntax worker
        let (syntax_tx, syntax_rx) = mpsc::channel::<ParseRequest>();
        {
            let msg_tx_clone = msg_tx.clone();
            std::thread::spawn(move || syntax_worker_loop(syntax_rx, msg_tx_clone));
        }

        let keymap = Keymap::with_bindings(load_default_keymap());

        Self {
            model: AppModel::new(window_width, window_height, file_paths),
            keymap,
            renderer: None,
            window: None,
            context: None,
            last_tick: Instant::now(),
            modifiers: ModifiersState::empty(),
            mouse_position: None,
            last_click_time: Instant::now(),
            last_click_position: None,
            click_count: 0,
            last_option_press: None,
            option_double_tapped: false,
            left_mouse_down: false,
            last_auto_scroll: None,
            drag_start_position: None,
            drag_active: false,
            msg_tx,
            msg_rx,
            perf: PerfStats::default(),

            syntax_tx,
        }
    }
}

// The worker: "latest request wins" pattern to reduce wasted work.
fn syntax_worker_loop(
    rx: Receiver<ParseRequest>,
    msg_tx: Sender<Msg>,
) {
    while let Ok(mut req) = rx.recv() {
        // Drain any queued requests; only keep the latest.
        while let Ok(next) = rx.try_recv() {
            req = next;
        }

        let highlights = run_syntax_highlighting(&req.text); // your parser

        let _ = msg_tx.send(Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::Updated {
            doc_id: req.doc_id,
            revision: req.revision,
            highlights,
        }));
    }
}
```

This worker:

- Ensures only the most recent queued request gets parsed (simple cancellation of queued work).
- Sends back `Syntax::Updated` for that request.
- Combined with revision checks in `update()`, stale results are safely ignored even if the worker was still parsing an older snapshot.

---

### 3. Wire syntax commands into `process_cmd`

Extend `process_cmd` in `app.rs`:

```rust
fn process_cmd(&self, cmd: Cmd) {
    match cmd {
        Cmd::None => {}
        Cmd::Redraw => {}
        Cmd::SaveFile { path, content } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::LoadFile { path } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::OpenInExplorer { path } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::OpenFileInEditor { path } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::Batch(cmds) => { for cmd in cmds { self.process_cmd(cmd); } }

        Cmd::ShowOpenFileDialog { allow_multi, start_dir } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::ShowSaveFileDialog { suggested_path } => { /* existing */ }
        Cmd::ShowOpenFolderDialog { start_dir } => { /* existing */ }

        Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse {
            doc_id,
            revision,
            delay_ms,
        } => {
            let tx = self.msg_tx.clone();
            std::thread::spawn(move || {
                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(delay_ms));
                let _ = tx.send(Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::ParseReady {
                    doc_id,
                    revision,
                }));
            });
        }

        Cmd::RunSyntaxParse {
            doc_id,
            revision,
            text,
        } => {
            // send to worker; no new thread per parse
            let _ = self.syntax_tx.send(ParseRequest {
                doc_id,
                revision,
                text,
            });
        }
    }
}
```

Nothing else in `app.rs` needs to know about syntax; `process_async_messages()` is already polling `msg_rx` and feeding `Msg` into `update()`.

---

### 4. Model changes: document revision + syntax state

In your `AppModel` / document model:

- Add a monotonically increasing `revision: u64` to each document.
- Add some syntax highlight state, e.g. `syntax: Option<SyntaxHighlightState>`.

Example:

```rust
// pseudo-code in model/document.rs

pub struct Document {
    text: Rope, // or String
    revision: u64,
    syntax: Option<SyntaxHighlightState>,
    // ...
}

impl Document {
    pub fn mark_edited(&mut self) {
        self.revision = self.revision.wrapping_add(1);
    }

    pub fn revision(&self) -> u64 { self.revision }

    pub fn set_syntax(&mut self, syntax: SyntaxHighlightState) {
        self.syntax = Some(syntax);
    }

    pub fn text_snapshot(&self) -> String {
        // convert Rope to String, or clone String
    }
}
```

---

### 5. Update logic: where to emit commands and handle syntax messages

In `update.rs`:

#### 5.1. On every text‑mutating `DocumentMsg`

At the end of handling any edit that changes the buffer (InsertChar, Delete*, Paste, etc.):

```rust
fn handle_document_msg(model: &mut AppModel, msg: DocumentMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    match msg {
        DocumentMsg::InsertChar(ch) => {
            let doc_id = model.active_document_id();
            let doc = model.document_mut(doc_id);
            doc.insert_char(ch);
            doc.mark_edited();

            Some(Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse {
                doc_id,
                revision: doc.revision(),
                delay_ms: 50,
            })
        }
        // similarly for other editing operations...
        _ => { /* existing handling, maybe also schedule DebouncedSyntaxParse */ }
    }
}
```

If you have lots of edit branches, factor this into a helper that you call whenever the document changed.

#### 5.2. When debounce timer fires (`Syntax::ParseReady`)

```rust
fn update(model: &mut AppModel, msg: Msg) -> Option<Cmd> {
    match msg {
        // existing arms...

        Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::ParseReady { doc_id, revision }) => {
            if let Some(doc) = model.document_opt(doc_id) {
                // stale: doc changed since this timer was scheduled
                if doc.revision() != revision {
                    return None;
                }

                let text = doc.text_snapshot();

                return Some(Cmd::RunSyntaxParse {
                    doc_id,
                    revision,
                    text,
                });
            }
            None
        }

        Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::Updated {
            doc_id,
            revision,
            highlights,
        }) => {
            if let Some(doc) = model.document_mut_opt(doc_id) {
                if doc.revision() != revision {
                    // parse for stale document contents; ignore
                    return None;
                }

                doc.set_syntax(highlights);
                return Some(Cmd::Redraw);
            }
            None
        }

        _ => { /* existing */ }
    }
}
```

This is where both debouncing and cancellation happen:

- Debouncing: we only ever start parsing if the document hasn’t changed in the last 50 ms (i.e., the `ParseReady` revision equals the current revision).
- Cancellation of stale parses: we drop results when their `revision` doesn’t match.

---

### 6. Why `std::sync::mpsc` + threads, not Tokio?

- You already use `std::sync::mpsc` and `std::thread::spawn` for file IO and dialogs; syntax highlighting fits that pattern.
- Winit’s event loop is synchronous; integrating a full async runtime correctly (especially on Windows/macOS) is non‑trivial and overkill for CPU‑bound work like parsing.
- Parsing is CPU‑bound; async runtimes mainly help with IO multiplexing, not heavy CPU.
- A single worker thread + MPSC gives you:
  - Backpressure (queue).
  - Simple “latest request wins” semantics (by draining `rx`).
  - No new dependencies.

Effort: M (1–3 hours) to wire this through `Msg`/`Cmd`/`update`, depending on how many edit paths you have.

---

3) Rationale and trade‑offs

- This design keeps all effects flowing through your existing Elm‑style `Msg → update → Cmd → process_cmd → Msg` pipeline, which keeps reasoning and testing simple.
- Debounce timers are modeled as commands; they’re easy to reuse for other features (e.g., linting) later.
- Revision numbers provide a cheap, robust cancellation primitive without needing complex cancellation tokens or cooperative checking inside the parser.
- Single long‑lived worker avoids spawning a thread on every parse, but is still simple; draining the channel gives you “cancel queued parses” behavior almost for free.

Alternatives (not chosen):

- Per‑parse thread: simpler but can spawn a lot of threads if the parse is expensive and the user types quickly.
- Immediate debounce in `App::about_to_wait`: workable, but spreads the logic between `update` and the outer loop instead of keeping it in the Elm “core”.
- Full async runtime (Tokio, async‑std): adds complexity and new constraints for little gain in this context.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- **Large documents / slow parser:** If parsing starts taking tens or hundreds of milliseconds, even a single worker may not be enough. Watch frame times and keypress latency.
- **Text snapshot cost:** Cloning the full document text per parse can be expensive for very large files; consider using a rope slice → iterator parser to avoid allocating a big `String`.
- **Thread‑safety of syntax state:** If `SyntaxHighlightState` is large, consider using `Arc` and cheap clones; keep it immutable after creation.
- **Version accounting:** Ensure every change to document contents increments `revision`; missing a path will produce subtle bugs where old results overwrite newer ones.

---

5) When to consider an advanced path

Revisit the design if:

- You add more background tasks (LSP, code analysis, indexing) that also need scheduling and cancellation.
- Syntax highlighting becomes very expensive (multiple languages, incremental parsing).
- You need parallel parses across many open files.

At that point, you might:

- Introduce a small generic job executor (thread pool) instead of a single worker.
- Use crossbeam channels for lower overhead.
- Or, if you start integrating LSP/process IO heavily, consider a single async runtime to coordinate that IO, while still using threads for CPU.

---

6) Optional advanced path (brief)

Advanced variant: job pool + priority/cancellation

- Create a small `JobExecutor` with N worker threads and a shared `crossbeam_channel::Receiver<Job>`.
- Each job has a `job_id` and `doc_revision`; workers cooperatively check an `AtomicU64` per document to know if they’re obsolete and can bail early.
- The scheduler (in `process_cmd`) pushes only the latest job per document, dropping older ones from a per‑doc queue.

This gives you stronger cancellation (workers can stop mid‑parse) and parallelism across documents but is likely unnecessary until you hit concrete performance issues.

For now, the single‑worker + `mpsc` + revision‑guarded messages is the simplest approach that fits your Elm architecture and all the constraints you described.
```

---

### Syntax Highlighting Implementation Plan

#### Architecture Overview

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        Main Thread                              │
│  Edit → bump revision → Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse              │
│            ↓ (50ms timer thread)                                │
│  Msg::SyntaxParseReady → check revision → Cmd::RunSyntaxParse  │
│            ↓ (syntax_tx channel)                                │
│  Msg::SyntaxUpdated → check revision → store → Cmd::Redraw     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              ↕ mpsc
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     Syntax Worker Thread                        │
│  ParseRequest → tree-sitter parse → extract highlights → send  │
│  (drains queue, only processes latest request per doc)         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

#### Phase 1: Core Infrastructure

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `Cargo.toml` | Add `tree-sitter`, `tree-sitter-yaml`, `tree-sitter-md`, `tree-sitter-rust` |
| `src/syntax/mod.rs` | Public exports |
| `src/syntax/highlights.rs` | `HighlightToken`, `LineHighlights`, `SyntaxHighlights`, `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` |
| `src/syntax/languages.rs` | `LanguageId` enum (PlainText, Yaml, Markdown, Rust, ...), extension detection |
| `src/syntax/parser.rs` | `ParserState`, embedded queries, `parse_and_highlight()` |
| `src/messages.rs` | Add `SyntaxMsg::ParseReady`, `SyntaxMsg::Updated` |
| `src/commands.rs` | Add `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse`, `Cmd::RunSyntaxParse` |
| `src/theme.rs` | Add `SyntaxTheme` with capture→color mapping |
| `themes/*.yaml` | Add `syntax:` section with colors for each capture |

#### Phase 2: Model & Async Integration

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `src/model/document.rs` | Add `revision: u64`, `syntax_highlights: Option<SyntaxHighlights>`, `language: LanguageId` |
| `src/runtime/app.rs` | Add `syntax_tx: Sender<ParseRequest>`, spawn worker thread |
| `src/runtime/app.rs` | Handle `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse` (spawn timer), `Cmd::RunSyntaxParse` (send to worker) |
| `src/update/mod.rs` | Add `Msg::Syntax` dispatch |
| `src/update/syntax.rs` | Handle `ParseReady` (revision check, emit RunSyntaxParse), `Updated` (store, redraw) |

#### Phase 3: Rendering

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `src/view/frame.rs` | Add `TextPainter::draw_with_highlights()` that applies per-token colors |
| `src/view/mod.rs` | Update editor line rendering to use highlights |

#### Phase 4: Language Support

| Language | Grammar Crate | Query Complexity |
|----------|---------------|------------------|
| YAML | `tree-sitter-yaml` | Simple (keys, values, comments) |
| Markdown | `tree-sitter-md` | Medium (headings, code, links) |
| Rust | `tree-sitter-rust` | Full (keywords, types, macros) |

#### Dependencies

```toml
tree-sitter = "0.24"
tree-sitter-yaml = "0.6"
tree-sitter-md = "0.3"
tree-sitter-rust = "0.23"
```

## Assistant

Comparing the two:

**~80% aligned** on architecture. Key differences:

| Aspect | Design Doc | My Plan |
|--------|-----------|---------|
| **Languages** | PHP, HTML, CSS, JS | YAML, Markdown, Rust |
| **Injection** | PHP+HTML layers | Deferred (simpler start) |
| **Commands** | Single `Cmd::ParseSyntax` with `EditInfo` | Two-step: `DebouncedSyntaxParse` → `RunSyntaxParse` |
| **Debouncing** | `pending_parse` + `check_pending_parse()` in tick | Timer thread spawned by command |
| **Staleness** | `EditInfo` for incremental + version | Revision numbers only (simpler) |
| **Queries** | External `queries/*.scm` files | Embedded in Rust initially |

**Same core concepts:**
- Background worker thread + mpsc
- `SyntaxHighlights` with `LineHighlights` → `HighlightToken`
- `LanguageId` enum with extension detection
- Theme integration via `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES`
- `Msg::Syntax*` variants for async results

**My plan simplifies** by:
1. Skipping incremental parsing (full reparse, fine for MVP)
2. Skipping injection (no multi-language files initially)
3. Using revision numbers instead of EditInfo tracking

Should I align more closely with the design doc (e.g., use `EditInfo`, external query files), or proceed with the simplified approach and iterate?

ParserState {\n382:     parsers: HashMap<LanguageId, Parser>,\n383:     trees: HashMap<DocumentId, Tree>,\n384:     queries: HashMap<LanguageId, Query>,\n385: }\n386: \n387: impl ParserState {\n388:   };\n394: \n395:         // Pre-initialize common languages\n396:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Php);\n397:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Html);\n398:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Css);\n399: \n400:         state\n401:     }\n402: \n403:     fn init_language(&mut self, lang: LanguageId) {\n404:         let (ts_lang, highlights_scm) = match lang {\n405:             LanguageId::Php => (\n406:                 tree_sitter_php::LANGUAGE_PHP.into(),\n407:                 include_str!(\"../../queries/php/highlights.scm\"),\n408:             ),\n409:             LanguageId::Html => (\n410:                 tree_sitter_html::LANGUAGE.into(),\n411:                 include_str!(\"../../queries/html/highlights.scm\"),\n412:             ),\n413:             LanguageId::Css => (\n414:                 tree_sitter_css::LANGUAGE.into(),\n415:                 include_str!(\"../../queries/css/highlights.scm\"),\n416:             ),\n417:             _ => return,\n418:         };\n419: \n420:         let mut parser = Parser::new();\n421:         parser.set_language(&ts_lang).expect(\"Language setup failed\");\n422:         self.parsers.insert(lang, parser);\n423: \n424:         if let Ok(query) = Query::new(&ts_lang, highlights_scm) {\n425:             self.queries.insert(lang, query);\n426:         }\n427:     }\n428: \n429:     /// Parse document and extract highlights\n430:     pub fn parse_and_highlight(\n431:         &mut self,\n432:         source: &str,\n433:         language: LanguageId,\n434:         doc_id: DocumentId,\n435:         edit: Option<&EditInfo>,\n436:     ) -> SyntaxHighlights {\n437:         let parser = match self.parsers.get_mut(&language) {\n438:             Some(p) => p,\n439:             None => return SyntaxHighlights::default(),\n440:         };\n441: \n442:         // Get or create tree\n443:         let old_tree = if let Some(edit_info) = edit {\n444:             self.trees.get_mut(&doc_id).map(|tree| {\n445:                 tree.edit(&edit_info.to_input_edit());\n446:                 tree.clone()\n447:             })\n448:         } else {\n449:             None\n450:         };\n451: \n452:         // Parse (incremental if old_tree provided)\n453:         let tree = match parser.parse(source.as_bytes(), old_tree.as_ref()) {\n454:             Some(t) => t,\n455:             None => return SyntaxHighlights::default(),\n456:         };\n457: \n458:         // Extract highlights using query\n459:         let highlights = self.extract_highlights(source, &tree, language);\n460: \n461:         // Cache tree for incremental parsing\n462:         self.trees.insert(doc_id, tree);\n463: \n464:         highlights\n465:     }\n466: \n467:     fn extract_highlights(\n468:         &self,\n469:         source: &str,\n470:         tree: &Tree,\n471:         language: LanguageId,\n472:     ) -> SyntaxHighlights {\n473:         let query = match self.queries.get(&language) {\n474:             Some(q) => q,\n475:             None => return SyntaxHighlights::default(),\n476:         };\n477: \n478:         let mut highlights = SyntaxHighlights {\n479:             lines: HashMap::new(),\n480:             version: 0,\n481:             language,\n482:         };\n483: \n484:         let mut cursor = QueryCursor::new();\n485:         let source_bytes = source.as_bytes();\n486: \n487:         for (match_, capture_idx) in cursor.captures(query, tree.root_node(), source_bytes) {\n488:             let capture = match_.captures[capture_idx];\n489:             let capture_name = &query.capture_names()[capture.index as usize];\n490: \n491:             // Map capture name to highlight ID\n492:             let highlight_id = HIGHLIGHT_NAMES\n493:                 .iter()\n494:                 .position(|&name| name == *capture_name)\n495:                 .unwrap_or(0) as HighlightId;\n496: \n497:             let node = capture.node;\n498:             let start = node.start_position();\n499:             let end = node.end_position();\n500: \n[... omitted lines 501 to 561 ...]\n562: \n563:         let old_end_line = rope.char_to_line(char_old_end.min(rope.len_chars()));\n564:         let old_end_col = char_old_end.saturating_sub(\n565:             rope.line_to_char(old_end_line)\n566:         );\n567: \n568:         // For new_end, we need to calculate based on new content\n569:         // This is approximate; proper impl would track actual new lines\n570:         let new_end_line = start_line; // Simplified\n571:         let new_end_col = start_col + new_text_len;\n572: \n573:         Self {\n574:             start_byte,\n575:             old_end_byte,\n576:             new_end_byte,\n577:             start_line,\n578:             start_col,\n579:             old_end_line,\n580:             old_end_col,\n581:             new_end_line,\n582:             new_end_col,\n583:         }\n584:     }\n585: }\n586: ```\n587: \n588: ---\n589: \n590: ## Rendering Integration\n591: \n592: ### Theme Extension\n593: \n594: ```rust\n595: // In src/theme.rs, add to EditorTheme\n596: \n597: pub struct EditorTheme {\n598:     pub background: Color,\n599:     pub foreground: Color,\n600:     pub cursor_color: Color,\n601:     pub selection_background: Color,\n602:     // ... existing fields ...\n603: \n604:     // NEW: Syntax highlighting colors\n605:     pub syntax: SyntaxTheme,\n606: }\n607: \n608: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n609: pub struct SyntaxTheme {\n610:     pub keyword: Color,\n611:     pub keyword_control: Color,   // if, for, return\n612:     pub function: Color,\n613:     pub function_builtin: Color,\n614:     pub string: Color,\n615:     pub number: Color,\n616:     pub comment: Color,\n617:     pub type_name: Color,\n618:     pub variable: Color,\n619:     pub variable_builtin: Color,  // $this, self\n620:     pub property: Color,\n621:     pub operator: Color,\n622:     pub punctuation: Color,\n623:     pub tag: Color,               // HTML tags\n624:     pub attribute: Color,         // HTML/XML attributes\n625:     pub constant: Color,\n626:     pub escape: Color,            // \\n, \\t in strings\n627: }\n628: \n629: impl SyntaxTheme {\n630:     pub fn get_color(&self, highlight_id: HighlightId) -> Color {\n631:         match HIGHLIGHT_NAMES.get(highlight_id as usize) {\n632:             Some(&\"keyword\") | Some(&\"keyword.function\") | Some(&\"keyword.return\")\n633:                 => self.keyword,\n634:             Some(&\"function\") | Some(&\"function.method\")\n635:                 => self.function,\n636:             Some(&\"function.builtin\")\n637:                 => self.function_builtin,\n638:             Some(&\"string\") | Some(&\"string.special\")\n639:                 => self.string,\n640:             Some(&\"number\")\n641:                 => self.number,\n642:             Some(&\"comment\")\n643:                 => self.comment,\n644:             Some(&\"type\") | Some(&\"type.builtin\")\n645:                 => self.type_name,\n646:             Some(&\"variable\") | Some(&\"variable.parameter\")\n647:                 => self.variable,\n648:             Some(&\"variable.builtin\")\n649:                 => self.variable_builtin,\n650:             Some(&\"property\") | Some(&\"tag.attribute\")\n651:                 => self.property,\n652:             Some(&\"operator\") | Some(&\"keyword.operator\")\n653:                 => self.operator,\n654:             Some(&\"punctuation.bracket\") | Some(&\"punctuation.delimiter\")\n655:                 => self.punctuation,\n656:             Some(&\"tag\")\n657:                 => self.tag,\n658:             Some(&\"attribute\")\n659:                 => self.attribute,\n660:             Some(&\"constant\") | Some(&\"constant.builtin\") | Some(&\"boolean\")\n661:                 => self.constant,\n662:             Some(&\"escape\")\n663:                 => self.escape,\n664:             _ => self.variable, // Default fallback\n665:         }\n666:     }\n667: }\n668: ```\n669: \n670: ### View Rendering\n671: \n672: ```rust\n673: // In src/view.rs, modify text rendering\n674: \n675: fn render_line_with_highlights(\n676:     buffer: &mut [u32],\n677:     font: &Font,\n678:     glyph_cache: &mut GlyphCache,\n679:     font_size: f32,\n680:     ascent: f32,\n681:     width: u32,\n682:     height: u32,\n683:     x: usize,\n684:     y: usize,\n685:     line_text: &str,\n686:     tokens: &[HighlightToken],\n687:     theme: &Theme,\n688:     default_color: u32,\n689: ) {\n690:     if tokens.is_empty() {\n691:         // No highlighting, use default color\n692:         draw_text(buffer, font, glyph_cache, font_size, ascent,\n693:                   width, height, x, y, line_text, default_color);\n694:         return;\n695:     }\n696: \n697:     let char_width = // ... get from renderer\n698:     let mut current_col = 0;\n699:     let mut token_idx = 0;\n700: \n701:     for (col, ch) in line_text.char_indices() {\n702:         // Find applicable token\n703:         while token_idx < tokens.len() && tokens[token_idx].end_col <= col {\n704:             token_idx += 1;\n705:         }\n706: \n707:         let color = if token_idx < tokens.len()\n708:             && col >= tokens[token_idx].start_col\n709:             && col < tokens[token_idx].end_col\n710:         {\n711:             theme.editor.syntax.get_color(tokens[token_idx].highlight).to_argb_u32()\n712:         } else {\n713:             default_color\n714:         };\n715: \n716:         // Draw single character with color\n717:         let char_x = x + (col as f32 * char_width) as usize;\n718:         draw_char(buffer, font, glyph_cache, font_size, ascent,\n719:                   width, height, char_x, y, ch, color);\n720:     }\n721: }\n722: ```\n723: \n724: ---\n725: \n726: ## Language Injection (PHP + HTML/CSS)\n727: \n728: PHP files often contain HTML, CSS, and JavaScript. Tree-sitter handles this via injection queries.\n729: \n730: ### Injection Query\n731: \n732: ```scheme\n733: ; queries/php/injections.scm\n734: \n735: ; HTML content between <?php ?> tags\n736: ((text) @injection.content\n737:  (#set! injection.language \"html\"))\n738: \n739: ; CSS in style attributes\n740: ((attribute\n741:   (attribute_name) @_name\n742:   (quoted_attribute_value (attribute_value) @injection.content))\n743:  (#eq? @_name \"style\")\n744:  (#set! injection.language \"css\"))\n745: \n746: ; JavaScript in onclick, onload, etc.\n747: ((attribute\n748:   (attribute_name) @_name\n749:   (quoted_attribute_value (attribute_value) @injection.content))\n750:  (#match? @_name \"^on\")\n751:  (#set! injection.language \"javascript\"))\n752: ```\n753: \n754: ### Multi-Layer Parsing\n755: \n756: ```rust\n757: pub struct SyntaxLayer {\n758:     pub language: LanguageId,\n759:     pub tree: Tree,\n760:     pub ranges: Vec<Range>,  // Where this layer applies\n761:     pub depth: usize,        // 0 = root, 1+ = injected\n762: }\n763: \n764: pub struct MultiLanguageDocument {\n765:     pub layers: Vec<SyntaxLayer>,\n766: }\n767: \n768: impl MultiLanguageDocument {\n769:     /// Get the most specific language at a byte position\n770:     pub fn language_at(&self, byte: usize) -> LanguageId {\n771:         self.layers\n772:             .iter()\n773:             .filter(|layer| {\n774:                 layer.ranges.iter().any(|r|\n775:                     byte >= r.start_byte && byte < r.end_byte\n776:                 )\n777:             })\n778:             .max_by_key(|layer| layer.depth)\n779:             .map(|layer| layer.language)\n780:             .unwrap_or(LanguageId::PlainText)\n781:     }\n782: }\n783: ```\n784: \n785: ---\n786: \n787: ## Performance Considerations\n788: \n789: ### Debouncing\n790: \n791: Don't parse on every keystroke:\n792: \n793: ```rust\n794: const PARSE_DEBOUNCE_MS: u64 = 50;\n795: \n796: // In document edit handler\n797: fn schedule_parse(&mut self, document_id: DocumentId) {\n798:     let now = Instant::now();\n799:     self.pending_parse = Some((document_id, now));\n800: \n801:     // In tick() or about_to_wait(), check if debounce elapsed\n802: }\n803: \n804: fn check_pending_parse(&mut self) -> Option<Cmd> {\n805:     if let Some((doc_id, scheduled_at)) = self.pending_parse.take() {\n806:         if scheduled_at.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(PARSE_DEBOUNCE_MS) {\n807:             return Some(Cmd::ParseSyntax { ... });\n808:         } else {\n809:             self.pending_parse = Some((doc_id, scheduled_at)); // Put back\n810:         }\n811:     }\n812:     None\n813: }\n814: ```\n815: \n816: ### Viewport-Limited Queries\n817: \n818: Only query visible lines + buffer:\n819: \n820: ```rust\n821: const HIGHLIGHT_BUFFER_LINES: usize = 50;\n822: \n823: fn extract_visible_highlights(\n824:     &self,\n825:     source: &str,\n826:     tree: &Tree,\n827:     language: LanguageId,\n828:     visible_start_line: usize,\n829:     visible_end_line: usize,\n830: ) -> SyntaxHighlights {\n831:     let buffer_start = visible_start_line.saturating_sub(HIGHLIGHT_BUFFER_LINES);\n832:     let buffer_end = visible_end_line + HIGHLIGHT_BUFFER_LINES;\n833: \n834:     // Convert line range to byte range\n835:     let start_byte = line_to_byte(source, buffer_start);\n836:     let end_byte = line_to_byte(source, buffer_end);\n837: \n838:     let mut cursor = QueryCursor::new();\n839:     cursor.set_byte_range(start_byte..end_byte);\n840: \n841:     // ... rest of extraction\n842: }\n843: ```\n844: \n845: ### Cancellation\n846: \n847: Cancel stale parses when document changes again:\n848: \n849: ```rust\n850: use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicBool, Ordering};\n851: use std::sync::Arc;\n852: \n853: struct ParseTask {\n854:     cancel_flag: Arc<AtomicBool>,\n855:     version: u64,\n856: }\n857: \n858: // Before spawning new parse, cancel previous\n859: if let Some(old_task) = self.current_parse.take() {\n860:     old_task.cancel_flag.store(true, Ordering::SeqCst);\n861: }\n862: ```\n863: \n864: ---\n865: \n866: ## File Structure\n867: \n868: ```\n869: src/\n870: ├── syntax/                  # NEW MODULE\n871: │   ├── mod.rs              # Public exports\n872: │   ├── parser.rs           # ParserState, background parsing\n873: │   ├── highlights.rs       # HighlightToken, SyntaxHighlights\n874: │   ├── languages.rs        # LanguageId, language detection\n875: │   └── injection.rs        # Multi-language layer handling\n876: ├── update/\n877: │   ├── mod.rs              # Add Msg::Syntax dispatch\n878: │   └── syntax.rs           # NEW: SyntaxMsg handlers\n879: ├── commands.rs             # Add Cmd::ParseSyntax\n880: ├── messages.rs             # Add SyntaxMsg\n881: └── theme.rs                # Add SyntaxTheme\n882: \n883: queries/                     # NEW: Tree-sitter query files\n884: ├── php/\n885: │   ├── highlights.scm\n886: │   ├── injections.scm\n887: │   └── locals.scm\n888: ├── html/\n889: │   ├── highlights.scm\n890: │   └── injections.scm\n891: ├── css/\n892: │   └── highlights.scm\n893: └── javascript/\n894:     ├── highlights.scm\n895:     └── locals.scm\n896: ```\n897: \n898: ---\n899: \n900: ## Dependencies\n901: \n902: ```toml\n903: # Cargo.toml additions\n904: \n905: [dependencies]\n906: tree-sitter = \"0.25\"\n907: tree-sitter-highlight = \"0.25\"  # Optional, if using high-level API\n908: \n909: # Language grammars\n910: tree-sitter-php = \"0.24\"\n911: tree-sitter-html = \"0.23\"\n912: tree-sitter-css = \"0.25\"\n913: tree-sitter-javascript = \"0.23\"  # For PHP injection support\n914: ```\n915: \n916: modifiers: SemanticModifiers,\n930: }\n931: \n932: /// Merge semantic tokens with syntactic highlights\n933: /// Semantic tokens take precedence where they exist\n934: pub fn merge_highlights(\n935: semantic boundaries\n940:     // ... merging logic\n941: }\n942: ```\n943: \n944: LSP message flow would be similar:\n945: \n946: ```\n947: Document Changed → Cmd::RequestSemanticTokens { uri, version }\n948:                       │\n949:                       ▼ (background LSP request)\n950: \n951: Msg::SemanticTokensReceived { uri, version, tokens }\n952:                       │\n953: `Cmd::ParseSyntax`\n967: - [ ] Implement basic `update_syntax()` handler\n968: - [ ] Add to `process_cmd()` for background parsing\n969: - [ ] Add tree-sitter dependencies\n970: \n971: ### Phase 2: parse_and_highlight(source, LanguageId::Php, None);\n1011: \n1012:     // \"function\" should be highlighted as keyword\n1013:     let line0 = &highlights.lines[&0];\n1014:     let keyword_token = line0.tokens.iter()\n1015:         .find(|t| t.start_col == 6 && t.end_col == 14)\n1016:         .expect(\"function keyword not found\");\n1017:     assert_eq!(HIGHLIGHT_NAMES[keyword_token.highlight as usize], \"keyword.function\");\n1018: }\n1019: \n1020: #[test]\n1021: fn test_incremental_parse() {\n1022:     let mut state = ParserState::new();\n1023:     let source1 = \"<?php $x = 1;\";\n1024: ## User

explain the scm query files, how does that stuff work.

## Assistant

Tree-sitter query files (`.scm`) use a Lisp-like syntax to match patterns in the syntax tree and assign "capture names" that map to highlight colors.

### How it works

**1. Tree-sitter parses source → AST**
```yaml
## keymap.yaml
bindings:
  - key: "cmd+s"
    command: SaveFile
```

Becomes a tree like:
```
(document
  (comment)
  (block_mapping
    (block_mapping_pair
      key: (flow_node (plain_scalar))
      value: (block_node (block_sequence ...)))))
```

**2. Query patterns match nodes and capture them**
```scheme
; highlights.scm for YAML
(comment) @comment
(plain_scalar) @string
(boolean_scalar) @boolean
(null_scalar) @constant.builtin
(anchor) @label
(alias) @label
(block_mapping_pair
  key: (_) @property)
```

**3. Captures map to theme colors**
- `@comment` → gray
- `@string` → green
- `@property` → blue (the `key:`, `command:` parts)

### Query syntax basics

| Pattern | Meaning |
|---------|---------|
| `(node_type)` | Match node by type |
| `(node_type) @name` | Capture matched node as `@name` |
| `(parent key: (child) @cap)` | Match child in specific field |
| `(_)` | Match any node type |
| `"literal"` | Match literal text |
| `(#eq? @cap "text")` | Predicate: capture must equal "text" |
| `(#match? @cap "regex")` | Predicate: capture must match regex |

### Example: Rust query

```scheme
; Keywords
["fn" "let" "mut" "pub" "use" "mod" "struct" "enum" "impl" "return" "if" "else" "match" "for" "while" "loop"] @keyword

; Function definitions
(function_item name: (identifier) @function)

; Function calls
(call_expression function: (identifier) @function.call)

; Types
(type_identifier) @type

; Strings
(string_literal) @string

; Comments
(line_comment) @comment
(block_comment) @comment

; Numbers
(integer_literal) @number
(float_literal) @number
```

The query engine runs these patterns against the AST, returns all matches with their byte positions, and we convert those to `HighlightToken` structs with line/column info.

Defines tokens, line highlights, and document-level syntax state.\n\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\n\nuse super::languages::LanguageId;\n\n/// Standard tree-sitter capture names mapped to theme colors.\n/// Index into this array is the HighlightId.\npub const HIGHLIGHT_NAMES: &[&str] = &[\n    \"attribute\",            // @attribute\n    \"boolean\",              // @boolean (true, false)\n    \"comment\",              // @comment\n    \"constant\",             // @constant\n    \"constant.builtin\",     // @constant.builtin (null, nil)\n    \"constructor\",          // @constructor (new Foo)\n    \"escape\",               // @escape (string escapes)\n    \"function\",             // @function\n    \"function.builtin\",     // @function.builtin (echo, print)\n    \"function.method\",      // @function.method\n    \"keyword\",              // @keyword\n    \"keyword.return\",       // @keyword.return\n    \"keyword.function\",     // @keyword.function (function, fn)\n    \"keyword.operator\",     // @keyword.operator (and, or)\n    \"label\",                // @label (anchors, aliases in YAML)\n    \"number\",               // @number\n    \"operator\",             // @operator\n    \"property\",             // @property\n    \"punctuation\",          // @punctuation (general)\n    \"punctuation.bracket\",  // @punctuation.bracket\n    \"punctuation.delimiter\", // @punctuation.delimiter\n    \"punctuation.special\",  // @punctuation.special\n    \"string\",               // @string\n    \"string.special\",       // @string.special (regex, heredoc)\n    \"tag\",                  // @tag (HTML tags, YAML tags)\n    \"tag.attribute\",        // @tag.attribute\n    \"text\",                 // @text (plain text in markdown)\n    \"text.emphasis\",        // @text.emphasis (*italic*)\n    \"text.strong\",          // @text.strong (**bold**)\n    \"text.title\",           // @text.title (headings)\n    \"text.uri\",             // @text.uri (URLs)\n    \"type\",                 // @type\n    \"type.builtin\",         // @type.builtin (int, string, bool)\n    \"variable\",             // @variable\n    \"variable.builtin\",     // @variable.builtin ($this, self)\n    \"variable.parameter\",   // @variable.parameter\n];\n\n/// Index into HIGHLIGHT_NAMES\npub type HighlightId = u16;\n\n/// A single highlighted span within a line\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct HighlightToken {\n    /// Start column (0-indexed, inclusive)\n    pub start_col: usize,\n    /// End column (exclusive)\n    pub end_col: usize,\n    /// Index into HIGHLIGHT_NAMES\n    pub highlight: HighlightId,\n}\n\n/// Highlight information for a single line\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\npub struct LineHighlights {\n    /// Tokens sorted by start_col\n    pub tokens: Vec<HighlightToken>,\n}\n\nimpl LineHighlights {\n    /// Get the highlight ID for a given column, if any\n    pub fn highlight_at(&self, col: usize) -> Option<HighlightId> {\n        for token in &self.tokens {\n            if col >= token.start_col && col < token.end_col {\n                return Some(token.highlight);\n            }\n            if token.start_col > col {\n                break; // tokens are sorted, no need to continue\n            }\n        }\n        None\n    }\n}\n\n/// Complete highlight state for a document\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct SyntaxHighlights {\n    /// Map of line number (0-indexed) → tokens\n    pub lines: HashMap<usize, LineHighlights>,\n    /// Document revision this corresponds to\n    pub revision: u64,\n    /// Primary language of document\n    pub language: LanguageId,\n}\n\nimpl Default for SyntaxHighlights {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            lines: HashMap::new(),\n            revision: 0,\n            language: LanguageId::PlainText,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl SyntaxHighlights {\n    /// Create new empty highlights for a language\n    pub fn new(language: LanguageId, revision: u64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            lines: HashMap::new(),\n            revision,\n            language,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Get highlights for a specific line\n    pub fn get_line(&self, line: usize) -> Option<&LineHighlights> {\n        self.lines.get(&line)\n    }\n\n    /// Get highlight tokens for a line, or empty slice if none\n    pub fn get_line_tokens(&self, line: usize) -> &[HighlightToken] {\n        self.lines\n            .get(&line)\n            .map(|lh| lh.tokens.as_slice())\n            .unwrap_or(&[])\n    }\n}\n\n/// Look up highlight ID by capture name\npub fn highlight_id_for_name(name: &str) -> Option<HighlightId> {\n    // Handle hierarchical names: \"keyword.function\" should match \"keyword.function\",\n    // but \"keyword\" should also match as a fallback\n    if let Some(pos) = HIGHLIGHT_NAMES.iter().position(|&n| n == name) {\n        return Some(pos as HighlightId);\n    }\n\n    // Try parent capture (e.g., \"keyword.function\" -> \"keyword\")\n    if let Some(dot_pos) = name.rfind('.') {\n        let parent = &name[..dot_pos];\n        if let Some(pos) = HIGHLIGHT_NAMES.iter().position(|&n| n == parent) {\n            return Some(pos as HighlightId);\n        }\n    }\n\n    None\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_highlight_id_lookup() {\n        assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"keyword\").is_some());\n        assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"keyword.function\").is_some());\n        assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"string\").is_some());\n        assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"nonexistent\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_line_highlights_at() {\n        let line = LineHighlights {\n            tokens: vec![\n                HighlightToken {\n                    start_col: 0,\n                    end_col: 5,\n                    highlight: 1,\n                },\n                HighlightToken {\n                    start_col: 10,\n                    end_col: 15,\n                    highlight: 2,\n                },\n            ],\n        };\n\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(0), Some(1));\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(4), Some(1));\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(5), None);\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(10), Some(2));\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(14), Some(2));\n        assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(15), None);\n    }\n}\n"
}

Maps file extensions to language IDs and provides language metadata.\n\nuse std::path::Path;\n\n/// Supported language identifiers\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Default)]\npub enum LanguageId {\n    #[default]\n    PlainText,\n    // Phase 1 languages\n    Yaml,\n    Markdown,\n    Rust,\n    // Phase 2 languages (web stack) - to be implemented\n    // Php,\n    // Html,\n    // Css,\n    // JavaScript,\n    // TypeScript,\n    // Phase 3 languages (common) - to be implemented\n    // Python,\n    // Go,\n    // C,\n    // Cpp,\n    // Json,\n    // Toml,\n}\n\nimpl LanguageId {\n    /// Detect language from file extension\n    pub fn from_extension(ext: &str) -> Self {\n        match ext.to_lowercase().as_str() {\n            // Phase 1\n            \"yaml\" | \"yml\" => LanguageId::Yaml,\n            \"md\" | \"markdown\" => LanguageId::Markdown,\n            \"rs\" => LanguageId::Rust,\n            // Future phases will add more\n            _ => LanguageId::PlainText,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Detect language from file path\n    pub fn from_path(path: &Path) -> Self {\n        path.extension()\n            .and_then(|ext| ext.to_str())\n            .map(Self::from_extension)\n            .unwrap_or(LanguageId::PlainText)\n    }\n\n    /// Get display name for the language\n    pub fn display_name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            LanguageId::PlainText => \"Plain Text\",\n            LanguageId::Yaml => \"YAML\",\n            LanguageId::Markdown => \"Markdown\",\n            LanguageId::Rust => \"Rust\",\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Check if this language has syntax highlighting support\n    pub fn has_highlighting(&self) -> bool {\n        !matches!(self, LanguageId::PlainText)\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_from_extension() {\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"yaml\"), LanguageId::Yaml);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"yml\"), LanguageId::Yaml);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"YML\"), LanguageId::Yaml);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"md\"), LanguageId::Markdown);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"rs\"), LanguageId::Rust);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"txt\"), LanguageId::PlainText);\n        assert_eq!(LanguageId::from_extension(\"unknown\"), LanguageId::PlainText);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_from_path() {\n        assert_eq!(\n            LanguageId::from_path(Path::new(\"config.yaml\")),\n            LanguageId::Yaml\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            LanguageId::from_path(Path::new(\"/path/to/README.md\")),\n            LanguageId::Markdown\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            LanguageId::from_path(Path::new(\"main.rs\")),\n            LanguageId::Rust\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            LanguageId::from_path(Path::new(\"no_extension\")),\n            LanguageId::PlainText\n        );\n    }\n}\n"
}

  "content": "//! Tree-sitter parser state and highlighting extraction\n//!\n//! Manages parsers, trees, and queries for syntax highlighting.\n\nuse std::collections::HashMap;\n\nuse tree_sitter::{Language, Parser, Query, QueryCursor, Tree};\n\nuse super::highlights::{\n    highlight_id_for_name, HighlightToken, LineHighlights, SyntaxHighlights,\n};\nuse super::languages::LanguageId;\nuse crate::model::editor_area::DocumentId;\n\n// Embedded query files\nconst YAML_HIGHLIGHTS: &str = include_str!(\"../../queries/yaml/highlights.scm\");\nconst MARKDOWN_HIGHLIGHTS: &str = include_str!(\"../../queries/markdown/highlights.scm\");\nconst RUST_HIGHLIGHTS: &str = include_str!(\"../../queries/rust/highlights.scm\");\n\n/// Thread-local parser state (tree-sitter parsers are !Sync)\npub struct ParserState {\n    /// Parser instances per language\n    parsers: HashMap<LanguageId, Parser>,\n    /// Cached syntax trees per document for incremental parsing\n    trees: HashMap<DocumentId, Tree>,\n    /// Compiled queries per language\n    queries: HashMap<LanguageId, Query>,\n}\n\nimpl ParserState {\n    /// Create a new parser state with initialized languages\n    pub fn new() -> Self {\n        let mut state = Self {\n            parsers: HashMap::new(),\n            trees: HashMap::new(),\n            queries: HashMap::new(),\n        };\n\n        // Initialize Phase 1 languages\n        state.init_language(LanguageId::Yaml);\n        state.init_language(LanguageId::Markdown);\n        state.init_language(LanguageId::Rust);\n\n        state\n    }\n\n    /// Initialize a language's parser and query\n    fn init_language(&mut self, lang: LanguageId) {\n        let (ts_lang, highlights_scm) = match lang {\n            LanguageId::Yaml => (tree_sitter_yaml::LANGUAGE.into(), YAML_HIGHLIGHTS),\n            LanguageId::Markdown => (tree_sitter_md::LANGUAGE.into(), MARKDOWN_HIGHLIGHTS),\n            LanguageId::Rust => (tree_sitter_rust::LANGUAGE.into(), RUST_HIGHLIGHTS),\n            LanguageId::PlainText => return,\n        };\n\n        // Create parser\n        let mut parser = Parser::new();\n        if let Err(e) = parser.set_language(&ts_lang) {\n            tracing::error!(\"Failed to set language for {:?}: {}\", lang, e);\n            return;\n        }\n        self.parsers.insert(lang, parser);\n\n        // Create query (may fail if query syntax is invalid)\n        match Query::new(&ts_lang, highlights_scm) {\n            Ok(query) => {\n                self.queries.insert(lang, query);\n            }\n            Err(e) => {\n                tracing::error!(\"Failed to compile query for {:?}: {}\", lang, e);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Parse document and extract highlights\n    pub fn parse_and_highlight(\n        &mut self,\n        source: &str,\n        language: LanguageId,\n        doc_id: DocumentId,\n        revision: u64,\n    ) -> SyntaxHighlights {\n        // Skip plain text\n        if language == LanguageId::PlainText {\n            return SyntaxHighlights::new(language, revision);\n        }\n\n        let parser = match self.parsers.get_mut(&language) {\n            Some(p) => p,\n            None => {\n                tracing::warn!(\"No parser for language {:?}\", language);\n                return SyntaxHighlights::new(language, revision);\n            }\n        };\n\n        // Get old tree for incremental parsing (if available)\n        // Note: For MVP we do full reparse; incremental will be added in Phase 2\n        let old_tree = self.trees.get(&doc_id);\n\n        // Parse the source\n        let tree = match parser.parse(source, old_tree) {\n            Some(t) => t,\n            None => {\n                tracing::error!(\"Parse failed for {:?}\", language);\n                return SyntaxHighlights::new(language, revision);\n            }\n        };\n\n        // Extract highlights\n        let highlights = self.extract_highlights(source, &tree, language, revision);\n\n        // Cache tree for future incremental parsing\n        self.trees.insert(doc_id, tree);\n\n        highlights\n    }\n\n    /// Extract highlight tokens from a parsed tree\n    fn extract_highlights(\n        &self,\n        source: &str,\n        tree: &Tree,\n        language: LanguageId,\n        revision: u64,\n    ) -> SyntaxHighlights {\n        let query = match self.queries.get(&language) {\n            Some(q) => q,\n            None => return SyntaxHighlights::new(language, revision),\n        };\n\n        let mut highlights = SyntaxHighlights::new(language, revision);\n        let mut cursor = QueryCursor::new();\n        let source_bytes = source.as_bytes();\n\n        // Run query and collect captures\n        for (query_match, capture_idx) in\n            cursor.captures(query, tree.root_node(), source_bytes)\n        {\n            let capture = &query_match.captures[capture_idx];\n            let capture_name = &query.capture_names()[capture.index as usize];\n\n            // Map capture name to highlight ID\n            let highlight_id = match highlight_id_for_name(capture_name) {\n                Some(id) => id,\n                None => continue, // Skip unknown captures\n            };\n\n            let node = capture.node;\n            let start = node.start_position();\n            let end = node.end_position();\n\n            // Handle multi-line nodes\n            if start.row == end.row {\n                // Single line token\n                let line_highlights = highlights\n                    .lines\n                    .entry(start.row)\n                    .or_insert_with(LineHighlights::default);\n\n                line_highlights.tokens.push(HighlightToken {\n                    start_col: start.column,\n                    end_col: end.column,\n                    highlight: highlight_id,\n                });\n            } else {\n                // Multi-line token: split across lines\n                let lines: Vec<&str> = source.lines().collect();\n\n                for row in start.row..=end.row {\n                    let line_highlights = highlights\n                        .lines\n                        .entry(row)\n                        .or_insert_with(LineHighlights::default);\n\n                    let (start_col, end_col) = if row == start.row {\n                        // First line: from start to end of line\n                        let line_len = lines.get(row).map(|l| l.len()).unwrap_or(0);\n                        (start.column, line_len)\n                    } else if row == end.row {\n                        // Last line: from start of line to end position\n                        (0, end.column)\n                    } else {\n                        // Middle lines: entire line\n                        let line_len = lines.get(row).map(|l| l.len()).unwrap_or(0);\n                        (0, line_len)\n                    };\n\n                    if start_col < end_col {\n                        line_highlights.tokens.push(HighlightToken {\n                            start_col,\n                            end_col,\n                            highlight: highlight_id,\n                        });\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Sort tokens within each line by start column\n        for line_highlights in highlights.lines.values_mut() {\n            line_highlights\n                .tokens\n                .sort_by_key(|t| (t.start_col, t.end_col));\n        }\n\n        highlights\n    }\n\n    /// Remove cached tree for a document (e.g., when document is closed)\n    pub fn remove_document(&mut self, doc_id: DocumentId) {\n        self.trees.remove(&doc_id);\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for ParserState {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self::new()\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_yaml_parsing() {\n        let mut state = ParserState::new();\n        let source = r#\"# Comment\nkey: value\nnumber: 42\nenabled: true\n\"#;\n        let doc_id = DocumentId(1);\n        let highlights = state.parse_and_highlight(source, LanguageId::Yaml, doc_id, 1);\n\n        assert_eq!(highlights.language, LanguageId::Yaml);\n        assert!(!highlights.lines.is_empty());\n\n        // Check that comment line has a comment token\n        if let Some(line0) = highlights.lines.get(&0) {\n            assert!(!line0.tokens.is_empty());\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_rust_parsing() {\n        let mut state = ParserState::new();\n        let source = r#\"fn main() {\n    let x = 42;\n    println!(\"Hello\");\n}\n\"#;\n        let doc_id = DocumentId(2);\n        let highlights = state.parse_and_highlight(source, LanguageId::Rust, doc_id, 1);\n\n        assert_eq!(highlights.language, LanguageId::Rust);\n        assert!(!highlights.lines.is_empty());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_plain_text_no_parsing() {\n        let mut state = ParserState::new();\n        let source = \"Hello, world!\";\n        let doc_id = DocumentId(3);\n        let highlights =\n            state.parse_and_highlight(source, LanguageId::PlainText, doc_id, 1);\n\n        assert_eq!(highlights.language, LanguageId::PlainText);\n        assert!(highlights.lines.is_empty());\n    }\n}\n"
}

In release builds, it's a direct dispatch with zero overhead.\n31: #[inline]\n32: pub fn update(model: &mut AppModel, msg: Msg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n33:     #[cfg(debug_assertions)]\n34:     {\n35:         update_traced(model, msg)\n36:     }\n37:     #[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]\n38:     {\n39:         update_inner(model, msg)\n40:     }\n41: }\n42: \n43: /// Inner update logic (no tracing)\n44: fn document::update_document(model, m),\n48:         Msg::Ui(m) => ui::update_ui(model, m),\n49:         Msg::Layout(m) => layout::update_layout(model, m),\n50:         Msg::App(m) =>   "path": "~/code/rust-editor/src/update/syntax.rs",
  "content": "//! Syntax highlighting update handlers\n//!\n//! Handles syntax-related messages for the Elm architecture.\n\nuse crate::commands::Cmd;\nuse crate::messages::SyntaxMsg;\nuse crate::model::AppModel;\n\n/// Debounce delay in milliseconds\npub const SYNTAX_DEBOUNCE_MS: u64 = 50;\n\n/// Handle syntax-related messages\npub fn update_syntax(model: &mut AppModel, msg: SyntaxMsg) -> Option<Cmd> {\n    match msg {\n        SyntaxMsg::ParseReady {\n            document_id,\n            revision,\n        } => {\n            // Check if document still exists and revision matches\n            let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&document_id)?;\n\n            // Skip if document has been edited since debounce started\n            if doc.revision != revision {\n                tracing::debug!(\n                    \"Skipping stale parse request: doc revision {} != request revision {}\",\n                    doc.revision,\n                    revision\n                );\n                return None;\n            }\n\n            // Snapshot the document content for parsing\n            let source = doc.buffer.to_string();\n            let language = doc.language;\n\n            Some(Cmd::RunSyntaxParse {\n                document_id,\n                revision,\n                source,\n                language,\n            })\n        }\n\n        SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted {\n            document_id,\n            revision,\n            highlights,\n        } => {\n            // Check if document still exists and revision matches\n            let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&document_id)?;\n\n            // Skip if document has been edited since parse started\n            if doc.revision != revision {\n                tracing::debug!(\n                    \"Discarding stale parse results: doc revision {} != result revision {}\",\n                    doc.revision,\n                    revision\n                );\n                return None;\n            }\n\n            // Store the highlights\n            doc.syntax_highlights = Some(highlights);\n            tracing::debug!(\n                \"Applied syntax highlights for document {:?}, revision {}\",\n                document_id,\n                revision\n            );\n\n            Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n        }\n\n        SyntaxMsg::LanguageChanged {\n            document_id,\n            language,\n        } => {\n            let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&document_id)?;\n\n            // Update language and clear old highlights\n            doc.language = language;\n            doc.syntax_highlights = None;\n\n            // Trigger a new parse\n            let revision = doc.revision;\n            Some(Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse {\n                document_id,\n                revision,\n                delay_ms: 0, // Immediate parse on language change\n            })\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Schedule a syntax parse for a document (call after document edits)\n///\n/// This returns a `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse` that should be included\n/// in the command returned from the document edit handler.\npub fn schedule_syntax_parse(model: &AppModel, document_id: crate::model::editor_area::DocumentId) -> Option<Cmd> {\n    let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&document_id)?;\n\n    // Skip plain text documents\n    if !doc.language.has_highlighting() {\n        return None;\n    }\n\n    Some(Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse {\n        document_id,\n        revision: doc.revision,\n        delay_ms: SYNTAX_DEBOUNCE_MS,\n    })\n}\n"
}

{
  "diff": "```diff\nIndex: ~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs\n===================================================================\n--- ~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs\toriginal\n+++ ~/code/rust-editor/src/runtime/app.rs\tmodified\n@@ -887,4 +887,39 @@\n             }\n         }\n     }\n }\n+\n+/// Syntax highlighting worker thread\n+///\n+/// Runs in a separate thread, receives parse requests, and sends back results.\n+/// Uses \"latest request wins\" pattern: drains queue and only processes most recent.\n+fn syntax_worker_loop(rx: Receiver<SyntaxParseRequest>, msg_tx: Sender<Msg>) {\n+    let mut parser_state = ParserState::new();\n+\n+    while let Ok(mut req) = rx.recv() {\n+        // Drain any queued requests, only keep the latest\n+        while let Ok(next) = rx.try_recv() {\n+            req = next;\n+        }\n+\n+        // Skip plain text (no highlighting)\n+        if !req.language.has_highlighting() {\n+            continue;\n+        }\n+\n+        // Parse and extract highlights\n+        let highlights = parser_state.parse_and_highlight(\n+            &req.source,\n+            req.language,\n+            req.document_id,\n+            req.revision,\n+        );\n+\n+        // Send results back to main thread\n+        let _ = msg_tx.send(Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted {\n+            document_id: req.document_id,\n+            revision: req.revision,\n+            highlights,\n+        }));\n+    }\n+}\n```",

#### Files Modified
- `Cargo.toml` - Added tree-sitter dependencies
- `src/lib.rs` - Added syntax module
- `src/messages.rs` - Added `SyntaxMsg` enum
- `src/commands.rs` - Added `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse` and `Cmd::RunSyntaxParse`
- `src/theme.rs` - Added `SyntaxTheme` with VS Code-like colors
- `src/model/document.rs` - Added `language`, `syntax_highlights`, `revision` fields
- `src/update/mod.rs` - Added syntax dispatch
- `src/update/app.rs` - Trigger syntax parse on file load
- `src/view/frame.rs` - Added `TextPainter::draw_with_highlights()`
- `src/view/mod.rs` - Use syntax highlighting in rendering
- `src/runtime/app.rs` - Added syntax worker thread and command handlers
- `docs/feature/syntax-highlighting.md` - Updated with implementation notes

#### Architecture
```
Edit → Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse → (50ms timer)
    → Msg::SyntaxParseReady → Cmd::RunSyntaxParse
    → (worker thread) → Msg::SyntaxUpdated → Cmd::Redraw
```
